- **2026-10-24** · ♥ 41 · ↻ 9 — **Article: Every way to earn on your Stables** [post](https://x.com/jupiter_earn/status/2114106811045552269)
  > Your stablecoins have more potential than waiting around for your next trade.
  > That's why Jupiter gives you over 7 different ways to earn on them:
  > 1. Jupiter Lend Earn Vaults
  > Jupiter Lend lets you deposit USDC, JupUSD, and more into lending pools to earn interest from borrowers.
  > In return, you receive a receipt token that is indicated by a jl-prefix. In the case of USDC, you'd get jlUSDC.
  > As you earn interest, the value of the jl-Token grows without needing to manually claim your earnings.
  > You can also access Lend through Jupiter Mobile, or just swap in and out of jlTokens directly.
  > 2. Auto-Earn in Jupiter Wallet
  > An integration that deserves its own mention is the Auto-Earn feature on Jupiter Wallet.
  > Toggle it on for specific stables, and your extension Wallet will automatically deposit your balance into Lend to start earning for you.
  > 3. DCA Earn
  > If you're spreading your purchases out over time through Dollar Cost Averaging, your funds can earn while they wait.
  > When you use Jupiter DCA with USDC, USDT, or JupUSD you can toggle on a feature to earn yield via Jupiter Lend in between your buys.
  > 4. Jupiter Lend Smart Vaults
  > Smart Vaults let paired assets serve as liquidity for Jupiter Lend AMM, earning fees from swaps.
  > In the case of Smart collateral, you supply a pair such as syrupUSDC-USDC and earn from a combination of lending interest, native token yield, and trading fees.
  > You can deposit either token, and Jupiter automatically composes the pair for you at the pool's current ratio.
  > 5. Lending or Longing through Jupiter Offerbook
  > Jupiter Offerbook gives you a couple different  options for your USDC, specifically.
  > Lend at a fixed rate: Fund an existing borrowing offer in USDC. If the borrower repays, you receive your funds back, plus interest. However, if they don’t repay after maturity, you can take their collateral.
  > Open a leveraged long: Deposit USDC to gain more exposure to a token's price action. To read more about leveraged longing with Offerbook, you can check out the docs.
  > 6. Swapping into yield-bearing stables.
  > When you click the 'yield' category on Jupiter’s stablecoin screener page, you can find a large list of yield-bearing stables and dollar-denominated tokens.
  > 7. Other ways to earn
  > For completeness, there are two additional options worth mentioning:
  > JLP: You can swap your stables into JLP, or mint it using supported assets such as USDC and JupUSD (when capacity allows).
  > JLP serves as the liquidity pool for Jupiter's JLP-based perps markets, and receives 75% of the fees generated. However, it also exposes you to a basket of assets including SOL, ETH, and BTC.
  > Perps Trading: Jupiter Perps Beta markets use USDC as the collateral asset, allowing you to long or short ZEC, Hype, JUP, SNDK, SPCX, or SKHYNIX.
  > But do note: The risk profile for perps is different compared to something like lending mentioned above. Market moves against your position can potentially cause a liquidation.
  > Activate your Stables
  > Whether you want hands-off background yield with Auto-Earn, fixed rates on Offerbook, or more active strategies via Perps, Jupiter has an outlet for your specific risk tolerance.
  > But regardless of what you choose, stop letting your stables sit on the sidelines.
  - links: https://offerbook.jup.ag/ · https://jup.ag/recurring · https://jup.ag/spot/stablecoins · https://docs.jup.ag/user-docs/earn/offerbook/multiply · https://jup.ag/perps/jlp-earn · https://jup.ag/lend/borrow/smart · https://docs.jup.ag/user-docs/trade/perps/beta-markets · https://jup.ag/lend/earn · https://docs.jup.ag/user-docs/manage/extension-wallet/auto-earn

- **2026-10-14** · ♥ 68 · ↻ 9 — **Article: Every way to earn on your JUP** [post](https://x.com/jupiter_earn/status/2110482464196166071)
  > ASR is live, with another 50M JUP for stakers to claim.
  > But across the Jupiverse, there's many ways to put your JUP to work.
  > Whether you're building a long-term position, looking for liquidity, or want to increase your exposure
  > Here's every way to earn on your JUP:
  > 1. Staking
  > You can Stake your JUP to earn Active Staking Rewards (ASR) while gaining voting power in the DAO.
  > Over 1.12 Billion JUP is currently staked, earning an estimated APY of 17.8%.
  > Your share of JUP rewards is time-weighted, meaning it depends how much you've staked and for how long.
  > Voting isn't required, but your stake also gives you a say whenever proposals come up.
  > 2. Borrowing Stables
  > Want access to liquidity while maintaining your JUP exposure?
  > Jupiter Lend lets you supply JUP as collateral and borrow USDC or USDT against it.
  > You can then use those funds elsewhere in DeFi, whether you're pursuing yield or preparing for your next trade.
  > You have to pay interest on what you borrow, and your position can experience a liquidation if your collateral value drops too far.
  > Just make sure to keep an eye on your position health.
  > 3. Borrow, Lend, or go Long via Offerbook.
  > Jupiter Offerbook gives you 3 different ways to participate in the JUP market.
  > Borrow against your JUP: Like Lend, you can borrow USDC from another user with JUP as collateral, at a fixed rate and for a set period.
  > There's no price based liquidations, but if your loan remains unpaid after maturity, the lender can claim your JUP.
  > Lend USDC against JUP: Take the lender's side of the equation. If the borrower repays , you receive your principal and any interest. If they don't, you can claim their JUP collateral.
  > Open a leveraged long: Deposit USDC to gain more exposure to JUP's price action.
  > Although this isn't technically 'earning on your JUP', it does allow you to earn on JUP's potential price appreciation.
  > To read more about leverage longing with Offerbook, you can check out the docs.
  > 4. Options in the Trade vertical
  > For completeness, its worth mentioning a couple other items:
  > If you're adding JUP to your portfolio, swaps from SOL or USDC via Jupiter Ultra carry a 0% platform fee.
  > Additionally, you can long or short JUP on Jupiter Perps with up to 20x leverage, using USDC as collateral.
  > Do note: Leverage increases your potential gains but also your potential losses, and positions can be liquidated if the market moves against you.
  > Give your JUP a role
  > Staking lets you grow your JUP position while participating in governance.
  > Borrowing gives you liquidity while keeping your JUP exposure.
  > And using USDC you can lend against JUP or trade based on its price movement.
  > When it comes to JUP, you have multiple tools at your disposal.
  > However you should be aware: If you don't stake your JUP, you won't be eligible for ASR.

- **2026-10-28** · ♥ 126 · ↻ 11 — **Article: Backpack Securities and Kamino Expand Credit for Tokenized Equities** [post](https://x.com/kamino/status/2115467476603433108)
  > Now live as collateral in the Backpack Market on Kamino, Backpack Securities tokenized equities give eligible holders access to USDC credit against tokens backed 1:1 by U.S.-listed shares, according to Backpack Securities.
  > Backpack Securities allows users to buy, sell, mint, and redeem tokenized securities backed 1:1 by real U.S.-listed stocks and ETFs on Solana. Kamino is the largest onchain lending protocol for tokenized equities by collateral deposited, with around $50 million in tokenized equities supplied as of October 2026. Through Kamino, eligible holders can borrow against those tokens while keeping price exposure to the underlying stock, subject to liquidation if the value of their collateral falls.
  > “Investors around the world want access to U.S. equities on their own terms, and ownership should carry the same utility onchain that it does in a brokerage account,” said Armani Ferrante, CEO of Backpack. “Backpack Securities is designed to preserve real ownership while making shares portable across Solana. Kamino’s infrastructure for  tokenized equities made it the natural venue, and integrating there gives our holders access to credit against the tokenized shares they already hold.”
  > 1:1-backed, Redeemable Through Backpack
  > According to Backpack Securities, each token is issued 1:1 against an underlying share held, and eligible Backpack account holders can redeem 1:1 for that share, subject to Backpack Securities’ terms of use. Backpack Securities states that the underlying holdings are security entitlements under New York UCC Article 8, cleared and custodied by RQD Clearing, LLC and Atomic Vault Securities, LLC. Both are SEC-registered and members of FINRA and SIPC. Backpack also states that customer securities are kept separate from the company’s creditor claims.
  > Redemption runs through a deposit to an eligible Backpack account. Dividends are reinvested into additional tokenized shares and credited to the holder’s wallet. Splits and other corporate actions are applied through a multiplier on the token mint.
  > Credit Infrastructure Built for Tokenized Equities
  > The Backpack Market is an isolated market with its own parameters and reserves, so bad debt or liquidity shortfalls in other Kamino markets do not pass through to it. Each tokenized equity on Kamino has its own loan-to-value, liquidation, and borrow-cap parameters, set by the manager of the market where it is listed based on the liquidity and oracle coverage. The market runs onchain around the clock, and users can deposit, borrow, repay and withdraw at any time.
  > Backpack Securities and Kamino Expanding Credit for Tokenized Equities
  > The Backpack Market expands loans backed by tokenized equities on Solana. Kamino continues to build credit infrastructure for a growing range of tokenized equities, so eligible users can access liquidity against their holdings without selling them, subject to liquidation risk. As Backpack Securities expands its supported assets, additional tokenized equities are expected to come to Kamino.
  > “Tokenized equities become useful when they can enter credit markets with transparent onchain risk parameters,” said Michael Weisz, CEO of Kamino. “Backpack Securities has built its model around 1:1 backing and redemption, and Kamino provides the infrastructure for those assets to serve as collateral. As more of the U.S. equity market moves onchain, we expect borrowing against tokenized shares to become one of the primary ways holders use them.”
  > Learn more about the Backpack Market here
  > About Backpack Securities
  > Backpack Securities is a securities business building infrastructure for the convergence of traditional and digital asset markets. Through a regulated brokerage platform and a complementary tokenization platform, Backpack Securities provides investors with access to real equity ownership and blockchain-native distribution of publicly traded securities within a single portfolio experience. Since launching in June 2026, tokenized stocks issued by Backpack Securities have recorded over $6 billion in cumulative onchain trading volume and attracted more than 560,000 holders.
  > To learn more about Backpack, visit backpack.exchange, X, or LinkedIn.
  > Disclaimer
  > Kamino is a protocol and institutional infrastructure layer. Where a Kamino market has a third-party market manager, that manager sets the market’s parameters; Kamino does not manage, endorse, or guarantee any market manager’s parameters or risk decisions, including Allez Labs’s. Backpack Securities tokens are issued by Backpack’s securities arm, not Kamino. Kamino does not issue, sell, custody, or redeem them; eligibility, redemption, custody, and corporate actions are governed by Backpack Securities’ terms of use. Protections described by Backpack Securities relate to the underlying holdings and do not cover tokens deposited on Kamino, smart-contract risk, or losses from liquidation. Kamino markets, including isolated markets, are subject to smart-contract and oracle risk. Using tokenized equities as collateral carries liquidation risk, including when markets for the underlying shares are closed. Please refer to Backpack Securities for full terms of use and eligibility: https://support.backpack.exchange/legal/general-legal/user-agreement. Targeted yield is not guaranteed and depends on loan performance; deposited capital may be partially or fully lost. Not a bank deposit, not insured. Subject to Kamino’s Terms of Service and geo-blocking — not available in restricted jurisdictions. Informational only, not investment, legal, or tax advice.

- **2026-10-23** · ♥ 62 · ↻ 3 — **Article: Consumer Auto Credit Scales 10x on Solana With Kamino Fixed Rates** [post](https://x.com/kamino/status/2113688733572562972)
  > Over $62M in fixed-rate borrowing and $77M in AUTO collateral were deployed through Multiply within one month.
  > Kamino Fixed Rates gives borrowers a defined cost of capital against tokenized assets on Solana. In the AUTO market, that financing has supported a tenfold increase in AUTO supplied within one month.
  > Since the first AUTO/wYLDS fixed-rate reserve opened, AUTO supplied on Kamino grew from $9M to $98M. Outstanding fixed-rate debt passed $62M, about 88% of all borrowing in the market.
  > AUTO gives holders exposure to U.S. consumer auto-loan financing through Hastra and Figure’s Prime marketplace. Holders can borrow wYLDS against AUTO for a 30-day fixed term, or use Multiply to borrow and acquire more AUTO in a single transaction.
  > For issuers, fixed-rate borrowing becomes a source of new supply. Multiply uses borrowed wYLDS to mint additional AUTO through Hastra, so demand for leverage turns into net-new issuance.
  > Variable Rates Limit Auto Credit Scalability
  > Borrowing costs determine how much yield AUTO holders retain when increasing their exposure through Multiply.
  > With variable-rate borrowing, those costs can rise while a position is open, reducing or eliminating the spread between AUTO’s yield and the borrowing rate.
  > Kamino Fixed Rates locks the borrowing rate for 30 days, giving holders a defined cost of capital for the term.
  > Fixed Rate Multiply Locks the Cost of Leverage on AUTO
  > The first AUTO fixed-rate reserve opened on September 21, 2026, at 5.30% with a 30-day term.
  > With Multiply, users deposit AUTO and borrow wYLDS to acquire more AUTO in a single transaction, increasing their exposure at a borrowing rate fixed for the term.
  > As demand grew, new reserves opened at 5.75%, 6.00%, and 6.25%. Each reserve carries its own fixed rate, so the market can add capacity without repricing loans already open.
  > Auto Rollover renews eligible loans into a new fixed term at maturity, so borrowers can keep positions open without refinancing manually.
  > $62.7M in Fixed Rate Loans in the First Month
  > Borrowers took out more than $10 million in fixed-rate loans in the first 72 hours and more than $40 million in the first two weeks.
  > Across the full AUTO market, deposits reached $169.7 million and total borrowing reached $70.9 million.
  > AUTO Collateral Grew 10x with Fixed Rates
  > Between September 21 and October 21, AUTO supplied on Kamino grew from $9.39 million to $98.53 million, an increase of $89.14 million.
  > The number of AUTO tokens supplied rose from 9.21 million to 95.97 million over the same period, so the growth came from new deposits rather than changes in AUTO's price.
  > The wider market expanded alongside the collateral base:
  > As of October 21, more than $77 million in AUTO was deployed across Fixed Rate Multiply positions, roughly 78% of all AUTO supplied on Kamino. Most of the new collateral went directly towards fixed-rate leverage strategies.
  > $12.7M Renewed Automatically Into New Fixed Terms
  > Auto Rollover has renewed $12.7M in AUTO fixed-rate debt, keeping borrowing active across terms without manual refinancing.
  > Renewals began on October 18, when the first 30-day loans entered their rollover window. By October 22, 68 positions had renewed, equivalent to approximately 20% of outstanding fixed-rate wYLDS debt.
  > Every renewal stayed in its existing reserve, rolling the full outstanding balance, including accrued interest, into a new 30-day term at the same fixed rate.
  > Auto Rollover can renew eligible loans up to three days before maturity, subject to available liquidity and the borrower’s rate settings. Holders can keep their AUTO exposure running with a defined cost of capital, without closing and rebuilding their positions at each expiry.
  > What AUTO Means for Tokenized Credit Issuers
  > Fixed-rate borrowing is driving AUTO’s growth on Solana. In one month, the AUTO Market grew over 8x in market size; fixed-rate loans accounted for 88% of all borrowing in the AUTO market.
  > Fixed Rate Multiply mints AUTO directly through Hastra, allowing holders to increase their exposure without relying on available DEX liquidity. Multiply helped drive over $40M in AUTO minting within two weeks, with Figure and Hastra reaching a new all-time high of $100M in AUTO circulation.
  > For tokenized credit issuers, this makes fixed-rate lending a distribution channel: holders borrow to acquire more of the asset, and that demand funds new issuance.
  > Fixed-rate borrowing is also live against ONyc in the OnRe Market, extending the same financing model to reinsurance-backed credit. Kamino’s next phase brings fixed-rate liquidity to more markets and maturities, giving more holders a defined cost of capital against their tokenized assets.

- **2026-10-20** · ♥ 144 · ↻ 18 — **Article: Establishing the Standard for Underwriting Cross-Chain Collateral With Chainlink CCIP** [post](https://x.com/kamino/status/2112590082553127419)
  > Kamino’s defense-in-depth framework combines real-time monitoring and market-level risk controls to protect the protocol. Our security review identified a proven track record and consistent security guarantees across chains as key reasons to support higher caps and LTVs for assets secured by Chainlink CCIP.
  > As Kamino onboards an increasing number of institutional tokenized assets, the infrastructure that powers them must meet the strict security standards required by capital markets. For Kamino, Chainlink’s Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol (CCIP) is the clear choice for supporting institutional tokenized assets as collateral across chains. Its security model meets the operational standards these assets demand, giving issuers and Kamino greater confidence as we scale.
  > The launch of Chainlink CCIP 2.0 reinforced this decision as it provides asset issuers even more control over cross-chain transfers while retaining the common verification baseline Kamino can underwrite. Issuers are able to add their own or third-party Cross-Chain Verifiers (CCVs), apply transfer policies, and configure confirmation thresholds; any added verifier must approve a transaction alongside CCIP’s default 16 independent, security-reviewed node operators. This lets issuers tailor controls to their assets while giving Kamino a consistent starting point for due diligence.
  > The focus on cross-chain security extends beyond lending markets, with asset issuers and major protocols across the industry choosing Chainlink CCIP’s secure-by-default infrastructure. In August, the Wyoming Stable Token Commission, issuer of the State of Wyoming's official stable token FRNT, announced its migration from LayerZero to Chainlink CCIP. Keith Lawhorn, the Commission's Chief Information Security Officer, then went further, publicly detailing the reasons behind the move.
  > CCIP is designed to address these risks at the infrastructure level. Independent operators observe and validate source-chain activity, while continuous monitoring helps detect abnormal behavior and configurable rate limits constrain the value that can move if something goes wrong. Together, these safeguards give Kamino a clearer security foundation to assess as assets expand across chains.
  > For a lending market, this is the core issue. A lender should not have to monitor the bridge behind every cross-chain asset it accepts, build its business around that function, or carry the liability when that infrastructure fails. Given the AUM cross-chain collateral now commands, that responsibility belongs with a cross-chain solution designed, and proven, to carry it.
  > Why Cross-Chain Tokens Are Harder to Underwrite
  > A single-chain token has one primary deployment to assess and monitor. Cross-chain assets add additional dependencies across each chain and cross-chain configuration.
  > First, exposure surface. The token's solvency depends on the weakest mint-and-burn relationship across every chain it touches. Unbacked supply created anywhere flows freely into every market that accepts it. The exploit need not happen where the lending market sits.
  > Second, the review never ends. A token can be Solana-native, pass full diligence, and earn its caps, then expand to Ethereum on a bridge configuration the lending market had no part in choosing. If that configuration fails review, the options are poor: cut caps after the fact, or unwind positions on an asset that was sound when onboarded. The risk profile changed without actual changes to its underlying economic substance.
  > Kamino’s approach is to evaluate the cross-chain configuration itself, measuring the risk each one carries and setting caps and LTVs against what that measurement supports.
  > How Kamino Evaluates Cross-Chain Collateral
  > Cross-chain collateral is evaluated through Kamino’s existing asset risk framework. Cross-chain dependencies can affect smart contract risk, depeg risk, counterparty risk, oracle pricing risk, and market risk, with those assessments informing LTVs, caps, isolation, and asset eligibility.
  > Data: oracle integrity and manipulation resistance, the foundation under every liquidation.
  > Cross-Chain: unbacked supply, undercollateralized wrappers, and any break between a token's price and its backing, including the off-chain verification and RPC layer underneath every bridge.
  > Application: LTVs, liquidation thresholds and bonuses, and caps configured so an upstream failure is not profitable to exploit.
  > Operational Security: the compromised keys and weak controls behind most large onchain losses, which override every technical guarantee above them and are by far the most difficult to quantify.
  > Containment: Solvency Signals as Circuit Breakers
  > A lending market cannot prevent an exploit on another chain. It can refuse to be where the consequences land. The direction Kamino is building toward is to monitor solvency in real time, consuming independent signals the way it consumes price oracles, each wired to a circuit breaker. This is live today across a subset of our cross-chain assets and is being extended deliberately. It reflects where cross-chain risk management is heading, and where we intend to stay ahead of it.
  > The signals are concrete. A gap between a token's DEX price and its redemption or peg price means the market has learned something the backing has not. A mismatch between supply and verifiable backing means unbacked tokens are circulating. An unexplained jump in supply is the onchain shadow of a minting event elsewhere. Any of these crossing a threshold is enough to pause the market on Solana, wherever the underlying event occurred, and without waiting to establish whose infrastructure failed.
  > Why Standardized Validation Makes This Possible
  > This containment depends on signals that are trustworthy and comparable across every token, and that is what separates the two models available to issuers.
  > A modular model, where each issuer configures its own verifier set per lane and can change it at any time, is close to impossible to underwrite at scale. Without an enforced minimum security floor, the conservative assumption is that most issuers default to settings that remain highly centralized, because growth and risk management pull in opposite directions. Continuous oversight is not feasible when security depends on a bespoke setup the issuer controls and can alter without notice, and each token becomes its own audit, repeated every time settings change or a chain is added.
  > A standardized model inverts this. When every lane is secured by the same set of vetted operators, the evaluation of one token carries over to the next. Diligence is done once at the standard level, which is what turns unknown risk into measurable risk, and measurable risk into confident caps and LTVs.
  > Among the providers building with this model, Chainlink's CCIP carries the track record Kamino weighs most heavily today. Kamino already uses Chainlink Data Streams as a primary source of RWA pricing within Scope, alongside other providers. Separately, CCIP provides a standardized cross-chain security baseline, with a default Committee Verifier consisting of 16 independent, security-reviewed node operators, CCIP combines a standardized verification layer with configurable rate limits and emergency controls designed to limit cross-chain exposure. What stands out is what Chainlink has sustained at scale: broad issuer adoption, consistent guarantees across chains, and is the only widely used bridge that has no funds-loss exploit on its record. Our preference is for the model first, and for the providers who prove they can deliver it in production. Chainlink has done that work, and our alignment reflects it.
  > What This Means in Practice
  > Greater uncertainty in a token's cross-chain posture demands a higher onboarding bar, and where risk cannot be validated, exposure comes down. Tokens on standardized, secure-by-default infrastructure, such as Chainlink CCIP, earn higher caps and LTVs because their risk can be quantified. Tokens on bespoke, per-lane configurations face heavier diligence, lower caps, and in some cases rejection or delisting, as a reflection of what can be measured rather than a judgment on the issuer.
  > …

- **2026-10-04** · ♥ 56 · ↻ 7 — **Article: Fixed Rate Multiply: Leveraged Yield with a Defined Cost of Capital** [post](https://x.com/kamino/status/2106784828313993267)
  > Kamino Fixed Rates bring a defined cost of capital and duration to onchain leverage, where financing has historically been dominated by variable-rate, open-term borrowing. This lets borrowers structure leveraged positions around known funding costs rather than utilization-driven rate changes.
  > With Fixed Rate Multiply, a user selects an asset and target leverage, and Kamino builds the position in one transaction. The debt comes from a fixed-rate reserve, locking the financing cost for the full term, while Auto Rollover can carry the position into another eligible fixed term at expiry without requiring manual refinancing.
  > The first Fixed Rate Multiply vault, AUTO/wYLDS, went live on September 21 with Figure and HastraFi. Since launch, the broader AUTO Market has scaled to $35M in fixed-rate loans, while AUTO supply has grown from $9M to $55M.
  > How Fixed Rate Multiply Works
  > Fixed Rate Multiply uses Kamino’s existing one-click leverage flow, but the borrow is originated at a fixed rate for a defined term instead of at a variable rate.
  > A user selects an asset and target leverage, and Kamino builds the position in a single transaction:
  > A flash loan provides the capital needed to reach the target leverage
  > The initial deposit and flash-borrowed funds are converted into the collateral asset
  > The full amount is deposited into Kamino as collateral
  > The debt asset is borrowed from a fixed-rate reserve and used to repay the flash loan
  > The result is a leveraged position with a financing cost that is fixed for the full term.
  > If any step fails, the transaction reverts and no position is created. Closing runs the same flow in reverse. The flash loan fee is 0.001% per transaction.
  > What Does a Fixed Rate Change?
  > Fixed Rate Multiply gives leveraged strategies a defined cost of capital over a defined period.
  > Instead of borrowing from a variable-rate reserve, the position borrows from a reserve configured for a specific asset, rate, and term. Once the loan is originated, that rate remains fixed through the end of the term, regardless of changes in utilization elsewhere.
  > Today, AUTO/wYLDS uses three 30-day wYLDS reserves at 5.30%, 5.75%, and 6%. Each reserve has its own available liquidity, and borrowers access that liquidity directly when opening a Fixed Rate position.
  > As Kamino expands Fixed Rates across additional terms and markets, these reserves can form the basis of a broader onchain yield curve.
  > Fixed-term borrowing also introduces different tradeoffs from open-term variable credit. Fixed rates can price at a premium to the prevailing variable rate, and closing during the initial term may incur an Early Repay Penalty to compensate lenders that committed capital for that duration. After the first rollover, positions can be closed without that additional penalty.
  > AUTO/wYLDS
  > AUTO is a yield-bearing token, backed by near-prime U.S. auto loans originated by Agora Data, structured through Figure Forge, and minted through HastraFi. The AUTO/wYLDS vault deposits AUTO as collateral, borrows wYLDS at a fixed rate, and loops the borrowed capital back into AUTO, maintaining USD-denominated exposure. Entry and exit route through Figure’s mint and redeem infrastructure rather than relying on secondary-market liquidity.
  > Auto Rollover: Carrying Positions Across Fixed Terms
  > Fixed-rate credit becomes significantly more useful onchain when borrowers can manage duration without rebuilding positions at every expiry.
  > Three days before the end date, a position enters its rollover window. If eligible fixed-rate liquidity is available, the loan can move into a new term automatically instead of requiring the borrower to close and manually refinance.
  > At expiry, Kamino follows the available path in order:
  > Roll into another eligible fixed term: If qualifying fixed-rate liquidity is available, the position continues into a new term.
  > Move into the variable-rate reserve where supported: If a fixed-rate rollover is unavailable, the debt can fall back to variable borrowing for the same asset in the same market.
  > Auto-repay: If neither path is available, the protocol begins repaying the debt from collateral according to the market’s configured Auto-repay mechanics.
  > Auto Rollover gives borrowers the defined cost of capital of fixed-term credit without turning every maturity into a manual refinancing event.
  > Users can disable rollover from the position interface, and the position lifecycle shows the active term, rollover window and expected expiry path.
  > Liquidations
  > A fixed rate removes financing-rate volatility during the active term. It does not remove collateral or liquidation risk.
  > AUTO/wYLDS has a maximum LTV of 78% and a liquidation LTV of 80%. A position opened near maximum leverage therefore has less room for relative movement between AUTO and wYLDS before reaching the liquidation threshold.
  > AUTO continues to accrue yield while interest accrues on the wYLDS debt. As long as AUTO’s yield remains above the fixed borrow rate and the relative value of the two assets remains stable, LTV should decline over the term. If that spread reverses or AUTO falls relative to wYLDS, LTV can increase.
  > Fixed Rate Multiply makes the financing cost predictable, but collateral performance, position sizing and liquidation risk still need to be managed throughout the term.
  > Defined Cost of Capital with Continuity Across Terms
  > Fixed Rates introduce a new function to onchain leverage: the ability to finance a position at a known cost for a defined period while maintaining continuity across maturities.
  > A borrower can calculate the financing spread at entry, size the position against that cost and hold through the term without the borrow rate repricing with utilization. As the term approaches expiry, Auto Rollover can carry the position into another eligible fixed term rather than forcing the borrower to rebuild it manually.
  > Fixed Rate Multiply packages that infrastructure into the same one-click experience already used for leveraged strategies across Kamino.
  > AUTO/wYLDS is the first Fixed Rate Multiply vault. From here, Kamino is expanding Fixed Rates across new markets and reserves, while adding new functionality around term selection, rollover and fixed-rate liquidity. As that infrastructure develops, a broader range of strategies will be able to access defined financing directly onchain.
  > Learn more: kamino.com/docs/products/borrow/fixed-rates
  > This is informational content and does not constitute financial advice, an offer, or a solicitation. Prospective depositors should review the full documentation and loan agreement, conduct their own due diligence, and consult with professional advisors before participating.
  > Kamino Lend involves risk, including potential loss of capital. Terms, rates, and features are subject to change and are not guaranteed. Subject to Kamino's T&Cs and geo-blocking — unavailable in restricted jurisdictions. Informational only, not investment, legal, or tax advice.
